=== SOURCE CODE (cpp) ===
#include <sstream>
#include <cctype>
#include <algorithm>
#include <cmath>
#include <stdexcept>

#include <string>
#include <unordered_map>
#include <vector>

class Words2Numbers {
public:
    Words2Numbers();
    std::string text2int(const std::string& textnum);
    bool is_valid_input(const std::string& textnum) const;

private:
    std::unordered_map<std::string, std::pair<int, int>> numwords;
    std::vector<std::string> units;
    std::vector<std::string> tens;
    std::vector<std::string> scales;
    std::unordered_map<std::string, int> ordinal_words;
    std::vector<std::pair<std::string, std::string>> ordinal_endings;
};


Words2Numbers::Words2Numbers() {
    units = { "zero", "one", "two", "three", "four", "five", "six", "seven", "eight", "nine", "ten",
             "eleven", "twelve", "thirteen", "fourteen", "fifteen", "sixteen", "seventeen",
             "eighteen", "nineteen" };

    tens = { "", "", "twenty", "thirty", "forty", "fifty", "sixty", "seventy", "eighty", "ninety" };

    scales = { "hundred", "thousand", "million", "billion", "trillion" };

    numwords["and"] = { 1, 0 };
    for (size_t idx = 0; idx < units.size(); ++idx) {
        numwords[units[idx]] = { 1, static_cast<int>(idx) };
    }
    for (size_t idx = 0; idx < tens.size(); ++idx) {
        numwords[tens[idx]] = { 1, static_cast<int>(idx) * 10 };
    }
    for (size_t idx = 0; idx < scales.size(); ++idx) {
        numwords[scales[idx]] = { static_cast<int>(std::pow(10, idx * 3)), 0 };
    }
    numwords["hundred"] = { 100,0 };
    ordinal_words = { {"first", 1}, {"second", 2}, {"third", 3}, {"fifth", 5}, {"eighth", 8},
                     {"ninth", 9}, {"twelfth", 12} };
    ordinal_endings = { {"ieth", "y"}, {"th", ""} };
}

std::string Words2Numbers::text2int(const std::string& textnum) {
    std::string text = textnum;
    std::replace(text.begin(), text.end(), '-', ' ');

    int current = 0, result = 0;
    std::string curstring;

    std::istringstream iss(text);
    std::string word;
    while (iss >> word) {
        auto it = ordinal_words.find(word);
        if (it != ordinal_words.end()) {
            int scale = 1, increment = it->second;
            current = current * scale + increment;
        }
        else {
            for (const auto& ending : ordinal_endings) {
                if (word.size() > ending.first.size() &&
                    word.compare(word.size() - ending.first.size(), ending.first.size(), ending.first) == 0) {
                    word = word.substr(0, word.size() - ending.first.size()) + ending.second;
                }
            }

            auto numword_it = numwords.find(word);
            if (numword_it == numwords.end()) {
                if (current > 0) {
                    result += current;
                    current = 0;
                }
                curstring += word + " ";
            }
            else {
                auto [scale, increment] = numword_it->second;
                if (scale == 1) {
                    current += increment;
                }
                else {
                    current *= scale;
                    result += current;
                    current = 0;
                }
            }
        }
    }

    if (current > 0) {
        result += current;
    }

    curstring += std::to_string(result);
    return curstring;
}

bool Words2Numbers::is_valid_input(const std::string& textnum) const {
    std::string text = textnum;
    std::replace(text.begin(), text.end(), '-', ' ');

    std::istringstream iss(text);
    std::string word;
    while (iss >> word) {
        if (ordinal_words.find(word) != ordinal_words.end()) {
            continue;
        }
        else {
            for (const auto& ending : ordinal_endings) {
                if (word.size() > ending.first.size() &&
                    word.compare(word.size() - ending.first.size(), ending.first.size(), ending.first) == 0) {
                    word = word.substr(0, word.size() - ending.first.size()) + ending.second;
                }
            }

            if (numwords.find(word) == numwords.end()) {
                return false;
            }
        }
    }

    return true;
}


=== PROMPT ===
You are an expert programmer. Translate the following cpp code to py.
- Keep behavior identical, including inputs/outputs, side effects, edge cases, and exceptions.
- Use idiomatic py only when it does not change behavior.

```cpp
#include <sstream>
#include <cctype>
#include <algorithm>
#include <cmath>
#include <stdexcept>

#include <string>
#include <unordered_map>
#include <vector>

class Words2Numbers {
public:
    Words2Numbers();
    std::string text2int(const std::string& textnum);
    bool is_valid_input(const std::string& textnum) const;

private:
    std::unordered_map<std::string, std::pair<int, int>> numwords;
    std::vector<std::string> units;
    std::vector<std::string> tens;
    std::vector<std::string> scales;
    std::unordered_map<std::string, int> ordinal_words;
    std::vector<std::pair<std::string, std::string>> ordinal_endings;
};


Words2Numbers::Words2Numbers() {
    units = { "zero", "one", "two", "three", "four", "five", "six", "seven", "eight", "nine", "ten",
             "eleven", "twelve", "thirteen", "fourteen", "fifteen", "sixteen", "seventeen",
             "eighteen", "nineteen" };

    tens = { "", "", "twenty", "thirty", "forty", "fifty", "sixty", "seventy", "eighty", "ninety" };

    scales = { "hundred", "thousand", "million", "billion", "trillion" };

    numwords["and"] = { 1, 0 };
    for (size_t idx = 0; idx < units.size(); ++idx) {
        numwords[units[idx]] = { 1, static_cast<int>(idx) };
    }
    for (size_t idx = 0; idx < tens.size(); ++idx) {
        numwords[tens[idx]] = { 1, static_cast<int>(idx) * 10 };
    }
    for (size_t idx = 0; idx < scales.size(); ++idx) {
        numwords[scales[idx]] = { static_cast<int>(std::pow(10, idx * 3)), 0 };
    }
    numwords["hundred"] = { 100,0 };
    ordinal_words = { {"first", 1}, {"second", 2}, {"third", 3}, {"fifth", 5}, {"eighth", 8},
                     {"ninth", 9}, {"twelfth", 12} };
    ordinal_endings = { {"ieth", "y"}, {"th", ""} };
}

std::string Words2Numbers::text2int(const std::string& textnum) {
    std::string text = textnum;
    std::replace(text.begin(), text.end(), '-', ' ');

    int current = 0, result = 0;
    std::string curstring;

    std::istringstream iss(text);
    std::string word;
    while (iss >> word) {
        auto it = ordinal_words.find(word);
        if (it != ordinal_words.end()) {
            int scale = 1, increment = it->second;
            current = current * scale + increment;
        }
        else {
            for (const auto& ending : ordinal_endings) {
                if (word.size() > ending.first.size() &&
                    word.compare(word.size() - ending.first.size(), ending.first.size(), ending.first) == 0) {
                    word = word.substr(0, word.size() - ending.first.size()) + ending.second;
                }
            }

            auto numword_it = numwords.find(word);
            if (numword_it == numwords.end()) {
                if (current > 0) {
                    result += current;
                    current = 0;
                }
                curstring += word + " ";
            }
            else {
                auto [scale, increment] = numword_it->second;
                if (scale == 1) {
                    current += increment;
                }
                else {
                    current *= scale;
                    result += current;
                    current = 0;
                }
            }
        }
    }

    if (current > 0) {
        result += current;
    }

    curstring += std::to_string(result);
    return curstring;
}

bool Words2Numbers::is_valid_input(const std::string& textnum) const {
    std::string text = textnum;
    std::replace(text.begin(), text.end(), '-', ' ');

    std::istringstream iss(text);
    std::string word;
    while (iss >> word) {
        if (ordinal_words.find(word) != ordinal_words.end()) {
            continue;
        }
        else {
            for (const auto& ending : ordinal_endings) {
                if (word.size() > ending.first.size() &&
                    word.compare(word.size() - ending.first.size(), ending.first.size(), ending.first) == 0) {
                    word = word.substr(0, word.size() - ending.first.size()) + ending.second;
                }
            }

            if (numwords.find(word) == numwords.end()) {
                return false;
            }
        }
    }

    return true;
}

```

- Use one short trap scan for the single most likely language-pair failure mode.

- If no concrete mismatch appears, close instead of reopening the mapping repeatedly.

- bouncing between partial code and repeated redesign;
- reopening the mapping for every local implementation detail;
- expanding one local translation doubt into a full restructuring cycle.

```
for (const auto& ending : ordinal_endings) {
    if (word.size() > ending.first.size() &&
        word.compare(word.size() - ending.first.size(), ending.first.size(), ending.first) == 0) {
        word = word.substr(0, word.size() - ending.first.size()) + ending.second;
    }
}
```
If a word ends with "ieth", after replacement to "y", the word length changes. Then the second iteration checks `word.size() > ending.first.size()` where ending.first is "th". For "twenty", len=6 > 2, but doesn't end with "th". So no. For a word like "twentieth" if it didn't match "ieth"? It does. What about a word like "twentiethth"? It ends with "ieth"? No, ends with "thth"? Actually "twentiethth" ends with "th" and also contains "ieth" earlier but not at end. So first condition false, second condition true -> "twentieth". Then numwords doesn't have "twentieth", so invalid. Good.

Now, let's think about the `numwords` dict initialization. In C++, they insert "and" first, then units, then tens, then scales, then hundred. The order doesn't matter for lookup. In Python, we can do:
```
self.numwords = {}
self.numwords["and"] = (1, 0)
for idx, unit in enumerate(self.units):
    self.numwords[unit] = (1, idx)
for idx, ten in enumerate(self.tens):
    self.numwords[ten] = (1, idx * 10)
for idx, scale in enumerate(self.scales):
    self.numwords[scale] = (10 ** (idx * 3), 0)
self.numwords["hundred"] = (100, 0)
```
But note: `self.tens` includes empty strings at idx 0 and 1. In Python, assigning `self.numwords[""]` twice: first (1,0), then (1,10). So final `self.numwords[""] = (1,10)`. Same as C++ (unordered_map overwrites). Good.

Alternatively, we can directly set scales values. But to preserve exact behavior including empty key, we can do the loop. It doesn't matter.

Need to ensure `units` and `tens` are lists of strings. `scales` list.

Now, let's write code.

Potential issue: In C++, `numwords` is `std::unordered_map<std::string, std::pair<int, int>>`. The pair is `{scale, increment}`. In Python, tuple `(scale, increment)`. Good.

Now, let's consider if `text2int` should be a method that modifies `curstring`? No.

Now, let's test with some examples manually.

Example: `text2int("one hundred")` -> "100". Our code: word "one" current=1. "hundred" scale=100 -> current=100 result=100 current=0. End result=100, curstring="100". Good.

Example: `text2int("one hundred and five")` -> "105". "and" scale=1 increment=0 -> current += 0. Good.

Example: `text2int("one cat two")` -> "cat 3"? Let's trace: "one" current=1. "cat" not in numwords: current>0 result=1 current=0; curstring="cat ". "two" current=2. End result=3, curstring="cat 3". Yes.

Example: `text2int("cat")` -> "cat 0". "cat" not in numwords: current=0 so no result; curstring="cat "; end result=0; curstring="cat 0". Good.

Example: `is_valid_input("one hundred")` -> True. `is_valid_input("one cat")` -> False. `is_valid_input("")` -> True.

Now, let's consider if there are any exceptions thrown? The C++ code doesn't throw. Python no.

Now, let's think about "short stable mapping path" and "trap scan". The most likely language-pair failure mode is the ordinal endings loop: In Python, if we use `for ending, replacement in self.ordinal_endings:` and modify `word`, it's fine. But if we use `word.endswith(ending)` and `len(word) > len(ending)`, we need to ensure that `ending` is a string. Good.

Another failure mode: In C++, `std::replace(text.begin(), text.end(), '-', ' ')` replaces all hyphens with spaces. Python `str.replace('-', ' ')` does same.

Another failure mode: Tokenization. C++ `istringstream >> word` skips leading whitespace and stops at whitespace. Python `split()` does same, but also splits on other whitespace characters like tabs/newlines. C++ `>>` also skips any whitespace, including tabs/newlines. So same.

Another failure mode: The `numwords` dict may contain empty string key. Python `split()` never returns empty string, so no issue. But if someone calls `text2int(" ")`, C++ text becomes " " (if no hyphen), istringstream >> no tokens, result "0". Python `" ".split()` returns [], result "0". Good.

Now, let's write the final code.

But wait: The prompt says "Use a short stable mapping path that preserves behavior." and "Output only the translated py code in a code block." So final answer should be just code block with Python code. No explanations.

Let's ensure code is idiomatic Python but behavior identical. We'll include class.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
class Words2Numbers:
    def __init__(self):
        self.units = [
            "zero", "one", "two", "three", "four", "five", "six", "seven", "eight", "nine", "ten",
            "eleven", "twelve", "thirteen", "fourteen", "fifteen", "sixteen", "seventeen",
            "eighteen", "nineteen"
        ]

        self.tens = ["", "", "twenty", "thirty", "forty", "fifty", "sixty", "seventy", "eighty", "ninety"]

        self.scales = ["hundred", "thousand", "million", "billion", "trillion"]

        self.numwords = {}
        self.numwords["and"] = (1, 0)
        for idx, unit in enumerate(self.units):
            self.numwords[unit] = (1, idx)
        for idx, ten in enumerate(self.tens):
            self.numwords[ten] = (1, idx * 10)
        for idx, scale in enumerate(self.scales):
            self.numwords[scale] = (10 ** (idx * 3), 0)
        self.numwords["hundred"] = (100, 0)

        self.ordinal_words = {
            "first": 1, "second": 2, "third": 3, "fifth": 5, "eighth": 8,
            "ninth": 9, "twelfth": 12
        }
        self.ordinal_endings = [("ieth", "y"), ("th", "")]

    def text2int(self, textnum):
        text = textnum.replace('-', ' ')

        current = 0
        result = 0
        curstring = ""

        for word in text.split():
            if word in self.ordinal_words:
                current += self.ordinal_words[word]
            else:
                for ending, replacement in self.ordinal_endings:
                    if len(word) > len(ending) and word.endswith(ending):
                        word = word[:-len(ending)] + replacement

                if word not in self.numwords:
                    if current > 0:
                        result += current
                        current = 0
                    curstring += word + " "
                else:
                    scale, increment = self.numwords[word]
                    if scale == 1:
                        current += increment
                    else:
                        current *= scale
                        result += current
                        current = 0

        if current > 0:
            result += current

        curstring += str(result)
        return curstring

    def is_valid_input(self, textnum):
        text = textnum.replace('-', ' ')

        for word in text.split():
            if word in self.ordinal_words:
                continue
            else:
                for ending, replacement in self.ordinal_endings:
                    if len(word) > len(ending) and word.endswith(ending):
                        word = word[:-len(ending)] + replacement

                if word not in self.numwords:
                    return False

        return True
```

=== TRANSLATED CODE (py) ===
class Words2Numbers:
    def __init__(self):
        self.units = [
            "zero", "one", "two", "three", "four", "five", "six", "seven", "eight", "nine", "ten",
            "eleven", "twelve", "thirteen", "fourteen", "fifteen", "sixteen", "seventeen",
            "eighteen", "nineteen"
        ]

        self.tens = ["", "", "twenty", "thirty", "forty", "fifty", "sixty", "seventy", "eighty", "ninety"]

        self.scales = ["hundred", "thousand", "million", "billion", "trillion"]

        self.numwords = {}
        self.numwords["and"] = (1, 0)
        for idx, unit in enumerate(self.units):
            self.numwords[unit] = (1, idx)
        for idx, ten in enumerate(self.tens):
            self.numwords[ten] = (1, idx * 10)
        for idx, scale in enumerate(self.scales):
            self.numwords[scale] = (10 ** (idx * 3), 0)
        self.numwords["hundred"] = (100, 0)

        self.ordinal_words = {
            "first": 1, "second": 2, "third": 3, "fifth": 5, "eighth": 8,
            "ninth": 9, "twelfth": 12
        }
        self.ordinal_endings = [("ieth", "y"), ("th", "")]

    def text2int(self, textnum):
        text = textnum.replace('-', ' ')

        current = 0
        result = 0
        curstring = ""

        for word in text.split():
            if word in self.ordinal_words:
                current += self.ordinal_words[word]
            else:
                for ending, replacement in self.ordinal_endings:
                    if len(word) > len(ending) and word.endswith(ending):
                        word = word[:-len(ending)] + replacement

                if word not in self.numwords:
                    if current > 0:
                        result += current
                        current = 0
                    curstring += word + " "
                else:
                    scale, increment = self.numwords[word]
                    if scale == 1:
                        current += increment
                    else:
                        current *= scale
                        result += current
                        current = 0

        if current > 0:
            result += current

        curstring += str(result)
        return curstring

    def is_valid_input(self, textnum):
        text = textnum.replace('-', ' ')

        for word in text.split():
            if word in self.ordinal_words:
                continue
            else:
                for ending, replacement in self.ordinal_endings:
                    if len(word) > len(ending) and word.endswith(ending):
                        word = word[:-len(ending)] + replacement

                if word not in self.numwords:
                    return False

        return True
